    41→    p: ({ children }) => {
    42→      // Check if any child is a block-level element (like Mermaid or CodeBlock)

    44→      // Simple heuristic: if any child is not a string/number, it *might* be a block.

    46→      return <p className="mb-4 leading-relaxed text-foreground">{children}</p>;

    49→    li: ({ children }) => <li className="text-foreground">{children}</li>,

    51→    blockquote: ({ children }) => <blockquote className="text-foreground border-l-accent italic">{children}</blockquote>,

    55→    table: (props) => {

> AGENT

The integration point is clear: the `img` renderer in `MarkdownRenderer.tsx` (lines 132–154). Here's my thinking on implementation:

## Approach Options

**1. `medium-zoom` (recommended)**
- Lightweight (~3.5kb gzipped), zero dependencies
- Zooms the image in-place with a smooth fade/scale effect — feels native to a reading experience
- CSS-customizable backdrop color

**2. `yet-another-react-lightbox`**
- Full lightbox: navigation arrows, captions, keyboard/swipe, thumbnails
- Heavier but appropriate if you want gallery-style navigation between post images

**3. `react-photo-view`**
- Pinch-zoom + swipe support, good for mobile
- Slightly more complex setup

---

## Recommended Plan: `medium-zoom`

Since `MarkdownRenderer` is a Server Component, we create a thin **`ZoomableImage` client component** that applies `medium-zoom` after mount:

1. Install `medium-zoom` (+ `@types/medium-zoom`)
2. Create `src/components/ZoomableImage.tsx` — client component with a `useEffect` that attaches `mediumZoom` to the underlying `<img>` element
3. Replace the `ExportedImage`/`<img>` returns in `MarkdownRenderer.tsx` with `<ZoomableImage>` wrappers

The backdrop color can be theme-aware (light/dark via CSS variable or `resolvedTheme`).

---

**Key tradeoff**: `medium-zoom` zooms a single image in isolation. If you want users to swipe through all post images like a gallery, go with `yet-another-react-lightbox` instead.

Which direction would you like to go?

> DEVELOPER

is this feature worth to implement?

> AGENT

Honest assessment: **probably not a high priority**.

**Arguments for:**
- Implementation cost is very low (~1 hour with `medium-zoom`)
- Good for technical diagrams, screenshots, or photo-heavy posts

**Arguments against:**
- Modern browsers already handle this: right-click → "Open image in new tab" or pinch-to-zoom on mobile
- `next-image-export-optimizer` already serves appropriately sized images — the rendered size is usually readable
- It's a polish feature, not a capability gap
- Adds a JS dependency for a primarily static site

**Compared to the other TODO items**, Image Zoom has the lowest impact-to-effort ratio. The other items on the Priority list deliver more meaningful value:
- **Dynamic OG** → directly improves how posts look when shared on social
- **SEO Boost (JSON-LD)** → structured data benefits search ranking
- **Breadcrumbs** → navigation clarity across all content types

If the blog is image-heavy (photography, architecture diagrams, charts), it's worth doing. If it's mostly prose with occasional images, I'd skip it or defer it to a "polish sprint" later.

**My suggestion:** Move it down the priority list and focus on Dynamic OG or SEO Boost first — both have broader user impact.

> DEVELOPER

OK, follow your suggestion.

> AGENT

Between the two I suggested, I'd go with **SEO Boost (JSON-LD)** first — it's more foundational and lower risk than Dynamic OG.

Here's why:
- **JSON-LD** is pure metadata added to `<head>` — no new runtime dependencies, no new routes, no image generation
- **Dynamic OG with Satori** requires a new `/api/og` route, font loading, and careful layout work — more moving parts
- JSON-LD benefits are durable (search engines read it on every crawl); OG images mainly matter at share time

**Proposed scope for JSON-LD:**
- `Article` schema for posts and book chapters
- `Book` schema for book index pages
- `BreadcrumbList` schema for post → series, book → chapter hierarchies

Want to proceed with this? I'll explore the current `<head>` / metadata setup first.

### A. **Post Detail Page** (`src/app/posts/[slug]/page.tsx`)

```typescript
// STATIC PARAMS GENERATION
generateStaticParams() {
  // Returns all post slugs from getAllPosts()
  // Checks if posts route is enabled (not using custom basePath)
  // If getPostsBasePath() !== 'posts', returns [{ slug: '_' }] (disabled)
  // In dev mode includes URL-encoded variants for Unicode paths
  // Production export keeps raw segment values
}

// METADATA GENERATION
generateMetadata({ params: Promise<{ slug: string }> }) {
  // Resolves post by slug (handles URL decoding, normalization)
  // Returns:
  // - title: `${post.title} | ${siteConfig.title}`
  // - description: post.excerpt
  // - openGraph: title, description, type: 'article', publishedTime, authors, images
  // - twitter: card: 'summary_large_image', title, description, images
  // - ogImage: post.coverImage (if valid) or siteConfig.ogImage fallback
}

// PAGE COMPONENT
export default async function PostPage({ params: Promise<{ slug: string }> }) {
  // Data retrieved at render time:
  const post = resolvePostFromParam(rawSlug)  // getPostBySlug() with fallbacks
  const relatedPosts = getRelatedPosts(slug)  // Up to 3 posts by tag/category
  const { prev, next } = getAdjacentPosts(slug)  // Older/newer posts
  const slugRegistry = buildSlugRegistry()  // Map of all slugs (posts/flows/notes/series)
  const backlinks = getBacklinks(slug)  // Wikilinks pointing to this post
  const seriesPosts = getSeriesPosts(post.series)  // If post belongs to series
  const seriesData = getSeriesData(post.series)  // Series metadata
  
  // Renders:
  // - PostLayout if layout !== 'simple' (with TOC, series sidebar, related posts, nav)
  // - SimpleLayout if layout === 'simple'
}
```

**Data Available:**
- Full `PostData` object: slug, title, date, excerpt, category, tags, authors, series, coverImage, featured, draft, latex, toc, externalLinks, readingTime, content, headings

## 3. DATA FUNCTIONS IN `/src/lib/markdown.ts`

### Core Query Functions:

**Posts:**
- `getAllPosts(): PostData[]` - All posts, filtered by draft/future status
- `getPostBySlug(slug: string): PostData | null` - Single post by slug (with date fallback search)
- `getRelatedPosts(currentSlug: string, limit: number = 3): PostData[]` - Related by tags/category
- `getAdjacentPosts(slug: string): { prev, next }` - Prev/next posts by date

**Series:**
- `getAllSeries(): Record<string, PostData[]>` - All series with their posts
- `getSeriesData(slug: string): PostData | null` - Series metadata (index.mdx)
- `getSeriesPosts(seriesName: string): PostData[]` - Posts in series
- `getSeriesAuthors(seriesSlug: string): string[] | null` - Explicit series authors
- `getSeriesCustomPaths(): Record<string, string>` - Custom URL prefix per series

**Books:**
- `getAllBooks(): BookData[]` - All books
- `getBookData(slug: string): BookData | null` - Single book with TOC
- `getBookChapter(bookSlug: string, chapterSlug: string): BookChapterData | null` - Single chapter

**Flows:**
- `getAllFlows(): FlowData[]` - All flow notes (YYYY/MM/DD structure)
- `getFlowBySlug(slug: string): FlowData | null` - Single flow
- `getFlowsByYear(year: string): FlowData[]` - Flows in year
- `getFlowsByMonth(year: string, month: string): FlowData[]` - Flows in month

**Notes:**
- `getAllNotes(): NoteData[]` - All knowledge base notes
- `getBacklinks(slug: string): BacklinkSource[]` - Wiki-style backlinks

**Utilities:**
- `buildSlugRegistry(): Map<string, SlugRegistryEntry>` - All slugs (posts, flows, notes, series) with URLs
- `getAuthorSlug(author: string): string` - Slugify author names
- `getPostUrl(post): string` - Resolve post URL respecting custom basePath

---

## 4. GENERATEMETADATA PATTERN

All pages follow this pattern:

```typescript
export async function generateMetadata({ params }: { params: Promise<{ slug: string }> }): Promise<Metadata> {
  const { slug } = await params;  // Await params Promise
  const data = getDataBySlug(slug);
  
  if (!data) {
    return { title: 'Not Found' };
  }
  
  const ogImage = data.coverImage && 
    !data.coverImage.startsWith('text:') && 
    !data.coverImage.startsWith('./')
    ? data.coverImage
    : siteConfig.ogImage;
  
  return {
    title: `${data.title} | ${resolveLocale(siteConfig.title)}`,
    description: data.excerpt,
    openGraph: {
      title: data.title,
      description: data.excerpt,
      type: 'article' | 'website',
      publishedTime: data.date,  // article type only
      authors: data.authors,     // article type only
      images: [{ url: ogImage, width: 1200, height: 630, alt: data.title }],
      siteName: resolveLocale(siteConfig.title),
    },
    twitter: {
      card: ogImage !== siteConfig.ogImage ? 'summary_large_image' : 'summary',
      title: data.title,
      description: data.excerpt,
      images: [ogImage],
    },
  };
}
```

**Key Points:**
- All `params` are `Promise<T>` in Next.js 15 - must await
- URL decoding: `decodeURIComponent(slug)` for Unicode support
- Fallback to `siteConfig.ogImage` for missing cover images
- External images (http/https, /, text:) are used as-is
- Relative images (./images/...) are resolved to `/posts/{slug}/...` or `/books/{slug}/...`

---

## 5. RENDER-TIME DATA AVAILABILITY

### At Page Component Runtime:

**Posts/Chapters/Flows/Notes/Series:**
- Full markdown content (already parsed from gray-matter)
- Frontmatter (title, date, excerpt, tags, authors, etc.)
- Extracted headings with IDs for TOC
- Reading time calculation
- Cover images (resolved to absolute URLs)
- Status flags (draft, featured, pinned)
- Related/adjacent items

**Series Pages:**
- Series metadata (if index.mdx exists)
- All posts in series (with optional manual ordering)
- Aggregated authors (explicit or from posts)
- Pagination control variables

**Book Pages:**
- Book metadata with TOC structure
- Chapter list with part grouping
- First chapter ID for CTA button
- Book body content

**Book Chapter Pages:**
- Full chapter data
- Previous/next chapter navigation metadata
- Chapter index for TOC highlighting

---

## 6. KEY OBSERVATIONS

1. **Static Generation with Dynamic Fallbacks:** `generateStaticParams` pre-generates all known routes. `dynamicParams: false` ensures 404 for unknown routes in production.

2. **URL Encoding Handling:** Special logic for Unicode slugs:
   - Dev mode includes both raw and URL-encoded variants
   - Production export uses raw segment values
   - Resolution attempts multiple normalization forms (NFC, NFD)

3. **Flexible Content Structure:**
   - Posts: flat files OR nested folders (with co-located images)
   - Books: chapters as flat files OR folders
   - Flows: strict `YYYY/MM/DD.md` structure
   - Series: folder-based with optional metadata file

4. **Draft & Future Post Handling:**
   - Production filters out drafts (`draft: true`)
   - `showFuturePosts` config controls visibility
   - Validated at both query time and render time

5. **Slug Resolution Strategy:** (especially `/posts/[slug]`)
   - Direct slug match
   - Date-prefixed filename fallback (if `includeDateInUrl: false`)
   - Unicode normalization fallback
   - Search across series folders

6. **Image Path Resolution:**
   - Absolute URLs (http/https): used as-is
   - Root paths (/): used as-is
   - Text placeholders (text:DG): used as-is
   - Relative paths (./): converted to `/posts/{slug}/...` or `/books/{slug}/...`

7. **Pagination:** All listing routes support pagination with configurable page sizes

8. **Backlinks & Slug Registry:** Built at render time, cached in production for performance

---

This architecture provides a flexible, statically-optimized digital garden with support for posts, series, books, flows, notes, and custom URL prefixes.

  icons: {

  },

**HTML Structure**:

- Line 141-147: Accessibility skip-to-content link
- Line 150: Selection styling with CSS variables
- Line 152: `<main id="main-content">` for landmark navigation

**Feed Alternates Logic** (lines 69-75):
Dynamically creates RSS/Atom feed discovery links based on `siteConfig.feed.format`:
```typescript
const feedAlternates = (() => {
  const { format } = siteConfig.feed;
  const types: Record<string, string> = {};
  if (format === 'rss' || format === 'both') types['application/rss+xml'] = '/feed.xml';
  if (format === 'atom' || format === 'both') types['application/atom+xml'] = '/feed.atom';
  return Object.keys(types).length > 0 ? { types } : undefined;
})();
```

export interface PostData {
  slug: string;
  title: string;
  subtitle?: string;
  date: string;                  // ISO format YYYY-MM-DD
  excerpt: string;
  category: string;
  tags: string[];

  series?: string;               // Series slug
  coverImage?: string;
  sort?: 'date-desc' | 'date-asc' | 'manual';
  posts?: string[];              // For series: post slugs
  featured?: boolean;
  pinned?: boolean;
  draft?: boolean;
  latex?: boolean;               // KaTeX math support
  toc?: boolean;                 // Table of contents
  externalLinks?: ExternalLink[];
  readingTime: string;           // e.g. "5 min read"
  content: string;               // Markdown/MDX content
  headings: Heading[];           // H2-H3 headings with IDs
  contentLocales?: Record<string, { content: string; title?: string; excerpt?: string; headings?: Heading[] }>;
}

  id: string;      // GitHub-slugged
  text: string;
  level: number;   // 2 or 3
}

export interface ExternalLink {
  name: string;
  url: string;
}

#### **Posts** (`/src/app/posts/[slug]/page.tsx:51-91`)

export async function generateMetadata({ params }: { params: Promise<{ slug: string }> }): Promise<Metadata> {
  const post = resolvePostFromParam(rawSlug);
  
  return {
    title: `${post.title} | ${resolveLocale(siteConfig.title)}`,
    description: post.excerpt,
    openGraph: {
      title: post.title,
      description: post.excerpt,

      images: [{ url: ogImage, width: 1200, height: 630, alt: post.title }],
      siteName: resolveLocale(siteConfig.title),

    twitter: {

      title: post.title,
      description: post.excerpt,
      images: [ogImage],
    },
  };
}

### Key Findings

1. **Metadata is well-structured for primary content** (posts, series, flows) with OG graph and Twitter cards
2. **Cover image handling is smart** - falls back gracefully from relative paths
3. **`metadataBase` from root layout** allows relative URLs in child routes (Next.js 13+)
4. **Feed discovery is automatic** - RSS/Atom link tags added via alternates
5. **No JSON-LD schema** - major SEO opportunity gap
6. **Incomplete metadata on secondary pages** - Tags, Authors, Book Chapters lack OG/Twitter
7. **No canonical URLs** in metadata (though Next.js may auto-generate from URL structure)
8. **i18n-aware** - locale resolution happens at runtime for title/description

Now I have a thorough understanding of the codebase. Let me compose the full implementation plan.

---

## Implementation Plan: JSON-LD Structured Data for Amytis

### Overview of Design Decisions

Before detailing the plan, here are the answers to all design questions:

**1. Schema types**

- Posts and flow pages: `BlogPosting` (a subtype of `Article`, more semantically precise for dated blog content; Google's Rich Results documentation treats `BlogPosting` as valid for article rich results)
- Books: `Book` (schema.org/Book, not `CreativeWork`; the book landing page is unambiguously a book)
- Book chapters: `Chapter` does not have mainstream search-engine rich-result support, so use `Article` with `isPartOf` pointing to the parent `Book` entity
- Breadcrumbs: `BreadcrumbList` with `ListItem` children

**2. Fields important for rich results**

Per Google's [Article rich results docs](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/appearance/structured-data/article):
- Required for `BlogPosting`: `headline`, `datePublished`, `author` (with `@type: Person`), `image`
- Recommended: `dateModified`, `publisher` (Organization), `description`, `url`, `keywords`, `mainEntityOfPage`
- For `BreadcrumbList`: `@context`, `@type`, `itemListElement` with `position`, `name`, `item`
- For `Book`: `@type: Book`, `name`, `author`, `url`, `datePublished`; `numberOfPages` is not available so omit

**3. Series title lookup**

In both `src/app/posts/[slug]/page.tsx` and `src/app/[slug]/[postSlug]/page.tsx`, `getSeriesData(post.series)` is already called and `seriesTitle` is already resolved before the return. The JSON-LD builder can simply receive `seriesTitle` as a parameter — no extra data-fetch is needed.

**4. Multiple schemas — one script tag or separate tags**

Use a single `<script>` tag per page with an array at the top level: `[{ BlogPosting schema }, { BreadcrumbList schema }]`. This is fully valid JSON-LD (the `@context` can appear on each object in the array, or the array can be wrapped with `@context` on the outer object using the `@graph` pattern). The `@graph` pattern is the cleanest approach:

```json
{
  "@context": "https://schema.org",
  "@graph": [
    { "@type": "BlogPosting", ... },
    { "@type": "BreadcrumbList", ... }
  ]
}
```

This means one `<script type="application/ld+json">` per page, containing the `@graph` wrapper. Pages with only one schema (e.g., Book landing page) still use `@graph` for consistency, making the output format uniform.

**5. TypeScript types**

Define explicit interfaces for each schema shape. Using `Record<string, unknown>` loses IDE assistance entirely. Lightweight interfaces with only the fields we set are sufficient — they do not need to be exhaustive against schema.org. A shared `SchemaBase` with `@type` and `@context` can anchor the union. The final output of each builder function is `object` so `JSON.stringify` works cleanly.

**6. Missing/optional fields**

Use the `undefined`-omission property of `JSON.stringify`: assign `undefined` to optional properties when absent, and they will be silently dropped. Never emit empty strings or empty arrays for optional fields. For `image`, fall back to `siteConfig.ogImage` (which already exists and is always set) — same logic as the existing `generateMetadata` functions.

---

### Architecture: `src/lib/json-ld.ts`

This is a pure TypeScript module — no React, no side effects, no imports from the data layer. It takes pre-resolved data as parameters and returns plain objects ready for `JSON.stringify`.

#### TypeScript interfaces

```typescript
// ── Internal schema shape interfaces ─────────────────────────────────────

interface SchemaOrganization {
  '@type': 'Organization';
  name: string;
  url: string;
}

interface SchemaPerson {
  '@type': 'Person';
  name: string;
  url?: string;
}

interface SchemaImageObject {
  '@type': 'ImageObject';
  url: string;
}

interface SchemaBlogPosting {
  '@type': 'BlogPosting';
  headline: string;
  description?: string;
  url: string;
  datePublished: string;
  dateModified: string;
  author: SchemaPerson[];
  publisher: SchemaOrganization;
  image: SchemaImageObject;
  keywords?: string;
  mainEntityOfPage: { '@type': 'WebPage'; '@id': string };
}

interface SchemaBook {
  '@type': 'Book';
  name: string;
  description?: string;
  url: string;
  datePublished: string;
  author: SchemaPerson[];
  publisher: SchemaOrganization;
  image?: SchemaImageObject;
}

interface SchemaArticle {
  '@type': 'Article';
  headline: string;
  description?: string;
  url: string;
  datePublished: string;
  author: SchemaPerson[];
  publisher: SchemaOrganization;
  isPartOf: { '@type': 'Book'; '@id': string; name: string };
}

interface SchemaBreadcrumbList {
  '@type': 'BreadcrumbList';
  itemListElement: Array<{
    '@type': 'ListItem';
    position: number;
    name: string;
    item: string;  // absolute URL
  }>;
}

interface SchemaGraph {
  '@context': 'https://schema.org';
  '@graph': Array<
    SchemaBlogPosting | SchemaBook | SchemaArticle | SchemaBreadcrumbList
  >;
}
```

#### Function signatures

```typescript
// Params types — all fields are pre-resolved by the caller

export interface PostJsonLdParams {
  post: {
    title: string;
    excerpt: string;
    date: string;
    authors: string[];
    tags: string[];
    coverImage?: string;
    series?: string;
  };
  postUrl: string;        // absolute URL, e.g. "https://example.com/posts/my-post"
  seriesTitle?: string;   // resolved series title when post.series is set
  seriesUrl?: string;     // absolute URL to the series page
  siteTitle: string;      // resolved (locale-stripped) site title
  siteUrl: string;        // siteConfig.baseUrl (no trailing slash)
  defaultOgImage: string; // absolute URL to fallback OG image
}

export function buildPostJsonLd(params: PostJsonLdParams): SchemaGraph

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  book: {
    title: string;
    excerpt?: string;
    date: string;

  };

  siteTitle: string;

}

export function buildBookJsonLd(params: BookJsonLdParams): SchemaGraph

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

export interface BookChapterJsonLdParams {

    title: string;

    slug: string;
  };
  book: {

    slug: string;

  };

  bookUrl: string;      // absolute URL to the book landing page
  siteTitle: string;

}

export function buildBookChapterJsonLd(params: BookChapterJsonLdParams): SchemaGraph

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

export interface FlowJsonLdParams {
  flow: {
    title: string;
    excerpt: string;
    date: string;
    tags: string[];
  };
  flowUrl: string;       // absolute URL, e.g. "https://example.com/flows/2026/03/05"
  siteTitle: string;

  defaultOgImage: string;
}

export function buildFlowJsonLd(params: FlowJsonLdParams): SchemaGraph
```

#### Internal helper

```typescript
function buildPublisher(siteTitle: string, siteUrl: string): SchemaOrganization {
  return { '@type': 'Organization', name: siteTitle, url: siteUrl };
}

function buildAuthors(names: string[]): SchemaPerson[] {
  // Produce at least one Person node. Falls back to site name if names is empty.
  if (names.length === 0) return [];
  return names.map(name => ({ '@type': 'Person', name }));
}

function resolveImageUrl(
  coverImage: string | undefined,
  defaultOgImage: string,
  siteUrl: string
): string {
  // Reject local paths (./images/…) and text placeholders
  if (
    coverImage &&
    !coverImage.startsWith('./') &&
    !coverImage.startsWith('text:') &&
    !coverImage.startsWith('/posts/') // local resolved paths have no SEO value without CDN
  ) {
    // External URL — use as-is
    if (coverImage.startsWith('http')) return coverImage;
  }
  // Fallback: absolute OG image URL
  const base = siteUrl.replace(/\/+$/, '');
  return defaultOgImage.startsWith('http') ? defaultOgImage : `${base}${defaultOgImage}`;
}
```

Note on `resolveImageUrl`: the existing `generateMetadata` functions already use this same logic (`!coverImage.startsWith('text:') && !coverImage.startsWith('./')` → use cover, else use `siteConfig.ogImage`). Our helper mirrors that pattern exactly, but also constructs an absolute URL (schema.org `image` must be absolute).

#### Exact JSON-LD schema structures

### Page Component Modifications

#### `src/app/posts/[slug]/page.tsx`

// Near top-level imports (add):
import { buildPostJsonLd } from '@/lib/json-ld';
import { resolveLocale } from '@/lib/i18n';

// Inside PostPage default export, after seriesTitle is resolved:
const siteUrl = siteConfig.baseUrl.replace(/\/+$/, '');
const postUrl = `${siteUrl}${getPostUrl(post)}`;
const seriesUrl = post.series ? `${siteUrl}/series/${post.series}` : undefined;

const jsonLd = buildPostJsonLd({
  post,
  postUrl,
  seriesTitle,
  seriesUrl,
  siteTitle: resolveLocale(siteConfig.title),
  siteUrl,
  defaultOgImage: siteConfig.ogImage,
});

// In the return statement, add before the layout component:
return (
  <>
    <script
      type="application/ld+json"
      dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: JSON.stringify(jsonLd) }}
    />
    <PostLayout ... />
  </>
);
```

Important: the `SimpleLayout` branch (line 109-111) does not need JSON-LD because `layout: "simple"` is used for static pages like "About", not blog posts. Skip JSON-LD injection for that branch.

### Full Implementation of `src/lib/json-ld.ts`

```typescript
import { getPostsListUrl } from './urls';

// ── Schema shape types ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

interface SchemaOrganization {
  '@type': 'Organization';
  name: string;
  url: string;
}

interface SchemaPerson {
  '@type': 'Person';
  name: string;
}

interface SchemaImageObject {
  '@type': 'ImageObject';
  url: string;
}

interface SchemaBlogPosting {
  '@type': 'BlogPosting';
  headline: string;
  description: string | undefined;
  url: string;
  datePublished: string;
  dateModified: string;
  author: SchemaPerson[];
  publisher: SchemaOrganization;
  image: SchemaImageObject;
  keywords: string | undefined;
  mainEntityOfPage: { '@type': 'WebPage'; '@id': string };
}

interface SchemaBook {
  '@type': 'Book';
  name: string;
  description: string | undefined;
  url: string;
  datePublished: string;
  author: SchemaPerson[];
  publisher: SchemaOrganization;
  image: SchemaImageObject | undefined;
}

interface SchemaArticle {
  '@type': 'Article';
  headline: string;
  description: string | undefined;
  url: string;
  datePublished: string;
  author: SchemaPerson[];
  publisher: SchemaOrganization;
  isPartOf: { '@type': 'Book'; '@id': string; name: string };
}

interface SchemaBreadcrumbList {
  '@type': 'BreadcrumbList';
  itemListElement: Array<{
    '@type': 'ListItem';
    position: number;
    name: string;
    item: string;
  }>;
}

type SchemaNode =
  | SchemaBlogPosting
  | SchemaBook
  | SchemaArticle
  | SchemaBreadcrumbList;

interface SchemaGraph {
  '@context': 'https://schema.org';
  '@graph': SchemaNode[];
}

// ── Helpers ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

function buildPublisher(siteTitle: string, siteUrl: string): SchemaOrganization {
  return { '@type': 'Organization', name: siteTitle, url: siteUrl };
}

function buildAuthors(names: string[]): SchemaPerson[] {
  return names.map(name => ({ '@type': 'Person', name }));
}

/**
 * Resolve an absolute image URL for structured data.
 * Returns undefined when no usable image is available (consumers can omit it).
 * Mirrors the cover-image logic in generateMetadata across all page components.
 */
function resolveAbsoluteImageUrl(
  coverImage: string | undefined,
  defaultOgImage: string,
  siteUrl: string
): string {
  const base = siteUrl.replace(/\/+$/, '');
  const useCover =
    coverImage &&
    !coverImage.startsWith('./') &&
    !coverImage.startsWith('text:') &&
    coverImage.startsWith('http'); // only external URLs are safe for schema.org
  if (useCover) return coverImage!;
  return defaultOgImage.startsWith('http')
    ? defaultOgImage
    : `${base}${defaultOgImage}`;
}

function wrapGraph(nodes: SchemaNode[]): SchemaGraph {
  return { '@context': 'https://schema.org', '@graph': nodes };
}

// ── Public param types ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

export interface PostJsonLdParams {
  post: {
    title: string;
    excerpt: string;
    date: string;
    authors: string[];
    tags: string[];
    coverImage?: string;
    series?: string;
  };
  postUrl: string;
  seriesTitle?: string;
  seriesUrl?: string;
  siteTitle: string;
  siteUrl: string;
  defaultOgImage: string;
}

export interface BookJsonLdParams {
  book: {
    title: string;
    excerpt?: string;
    date: string;
    authors: string[];
    coverImage?: string;
    slug: string;
  };
  bookUrl: string;
  siteTitle: string;
  siteUrl: string;
  defaultOgImage: string;
}

export interface BookChapterJsonLdParams {
  chapter: {
    title: string;
    excerpt?: string;
    slug: string;
  };
  book: {
    title: string;
    slug: string;
    date: string;
    authors: string[];
  };
  chapterUrl: string;
  bookUrl: string;
  siteTitle: string;
  siteUrl: string;
}

export interface FlowJsonLdParams {
  flow: {
    title: string;
    excerpt: string;
    date: string;
    tags: string[];
  };
  flowUrl: string;
  siteTitle: string;
  siteUrl: string;
  defaultOgImage: string;
}

// ── Builders ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

export function buildPostJsonLd(params: PostJsonLdParams): SchemaGraph {
  const { post, postUrl, seriesTitle, seriesUrl, siteTitle, siteUrl, defaultOgImage } = params;
  const base = siteUrl.replace(/\/+$/, '');

  const article: SchemaBlogPosting = {
    '@type': 'BlogPosting',
    headline: post.title,
    description: post.excerpt || undefined,
    url: postUrl,
    datePublished: post.date,
    dateModified: post.date,
    author: buildAuthors(post.authors),
    publisher: buildPublisher(siteTitle, base),
    image: {
      '@type': 'ImageObject',
      url: resolveAbsoluteImageUrl(post.coverImage, defaultOgImage, base),
    },
    keywords: post.tags.length > 0 ? post.tags.join(', ') : undefined,
    mainEntityOfPage: { '@type': 'WebPage', '@id': postUrl },
  };

  // Build breadcrumb: Home → [Series or Posts] → Post
  const breadcrumbItems: SchemaBreadcrumbList['itemListElement'] = [
    { '@type': 'ListItem', position: 1, name: 'Home', item: base },
  ];

  if (seriesTitle && seriesUrl) {
    breadcrumbItems.push({
      '@type': 'ListItem',
      position: 2,
      name: seriesTitle,
      item: seriesUrl,
    });
  } else {
    breadcrumbItems.push({
      '@type': 'ListItem',
      position: 2,
      name: 'Posts',
      item: `${base}${getPostsListUrl()}`,
    });
  }

  breadcrumbItems.push({
    '@type': 'ListItem',
    position: breadcrumbItems.length + 1,
    name: post.title,
    item: postUrl,
  });

  const breadcrumb: SchemaBreadcrumbList = {
    '@type': 'BreadcrumbList',
    itemListElement: breadcrumbItems,
  };

  return wrapGraph([article, breadcrumb]);
}

export function buildBookJsonLd(params: BookJsonLdParams): SchemaGraph {
  const { book, bookUrl, siteTitle, siteUrl, defaultOgImage } = params;
  const base = siteUrl.replace(/\/+$/, '');

  const imageUrl = resolveAbsoluteImageUrl(book.coverImage, defaultOgImage, base);

  const bookSchema: SchemaBook = {
    '@type': 'Book',
    name: book.title,
    description: book.excerpt || undefined,
    url: bookUrl,
    datePublished: book.date,
    author: buildAuthors(book.authors),
    publisher: buildPublisher(siteTitle, base),
    image: imageUrl ? { '@type': 'ImageObject', url: imageUrl } : undefined,
  };

  const breadcrumb: SchemaBreadcrumbList = {
    '@type': 'BreadcrumbList',
    itemListElement: [
      { '@type': 'ListItem', position: 1, name: 'Home', item: base },
      { '@type': 'ListItem', position: 2, name: 'Books', item: `${base}/books` },
      { '@type': 'ListItem', position: 3, name: book.title, item: bookUrl },
    ],
  };

  return wrapGraph([bookSchema, breadcrumb]);
}

export function buildBookChapterJsonLd(params: BookChapterJsonLdParams): SchemaGraph {
  const { chapter, book, chapterUrl, bookUrl, siteTitle, siteUrl } = params;
  const base = siteUrl.replace(/\/+$/, '');

  const article: SchemaArticle = {
    '@type': 'Article',
    headline: chapter.title,
    description: chapter.excerpt || undefined,
    url: chapterUrl,
    datePublished: book.date,
    author: buildAuthors(book.authors),
    publisher: buildPublisher(siteTitle, base),
    isPartOf: { '@type': 'Book', '@id': bookUrl, name: book.title },
  };

  const breadcrumb: SchemaBreadcrumbList = {
    '@type': 'BreadcrumbList',
    itemListElement: [
      { '@type': 'ListItem', position: 1, name: 'Home', item: base },
      { '@type': 'ListItem', position: 2, name: 'Books', item: `${base}/books` },
      { '@type': 'ListItem', position: 3, name: book.title, item: bookUrl },
      { '@type': 'ListItem', position: 4, name: chapter.title, item: chapterUrl },
    ],
  };

  return wrapGraph([article, breadcrumb]);
}

export function buildFlowJsonLd(params: FlowJsonLdParams): SchemaGraph {
  const { flow, flowUrl, siteTitle, siteUrl, defaultOgImage } = params;
  const base = siteUrl.replace(/\/+$/, '');

  const article: SchemaBlogPosting = {
    '@type': 'BlogPosting',
    headline: flow.title,
    description: flow.excerpt || undefined,
    url: flowUrl,
    datePublished: flow.date,
    dateModified: flow.date,
    author: [],    // flows have no author field in FlowData
    publisher: buildPublisher(siteTitle, base),
    image: {
      '@type': 'ImageObject',
      url: resolveAbsoluteImageUrl(undefined, defaultOgImage, base),
    },
    keywords: flow.tags.length > 0 ? flow.tags.join(', ') : undefined,
    mainEntityOfPage: { '@type': 'WebPage', '@id': flowUrl },
  };

  return wrapGraph([article]);
}
```

---

### Edge Cases

| Scenario | Handling |
|---|---|
| Post has no authors (`authors: []`) | `buildAuthors([])` returns `[]` — Google still processes the schema, and `siteConfig.posts.authors.default` ensures authors is populated by the data layer before reaching JSON-LD |
| Post has no cover image | `resolveAbsoluteImageUrl` falls back to `defaultOgImage` — always produces a valid URL |
| Post has a local cover image (`/posts/slug/images/cover.jpg`) | Excluded from schema (not externally accessible without CDN); falls back to `defaultOgImage` |
| Post has no excerpt | `post.excerpt` is always populated by `generateExcerpt()` in `markdown.ts`; no null risk |
| Post has no tags | `keywords` field is `undefined` — `JSON.stringify` omits it |
| Post belongs to series but `getSeriesData` returns null | Caller passes `seriesTitle: undefined`; breadcrumb falls back to Posts listing |
| Book has `text:` cover image | `resolveAbsoluteImageUrl` excludes it; book schema `image` will be the OG fallback |
| Book chapter has no excerpt | `description` is `undefined` — omitted by `JSON.stringify` |
| Flow title falls back to date string | Still valid as `headline`; minimal but correct |
| `siteConfig.baseUrl` has trailing slash | `base = siteUrl.replace(/\/+$/, '')` normalizes it everywhere |

---

### Implementation Sequence

1. Create `src/lib/json-ld.ts` with all interfaces, helpers, and builder functions.
2. Modify `src/app/posts/[slug]/page.tsx` — add import, build `jsonLd`, wrap return in fragment with `<script>`.
3. Modify `src/app/[slug]/[postSlug]/page.tsx` — identical changes.
4. Modify `src/app/books/[slug]/page.tsx` — add import, build `jsonLd`, wrap return.
5. Modify `src/app/books/[slug]/[chapter]/page.tsx` — add import, build `jsonLd`, wrap `<BookLayout>` in fragment.
6. Modify `src/app/flows/[year]/[month]/[day]/page.tsx` — add import, build `jsonLd`, wrap return.
7. Validate with Google's Rich Results Test after a `bun run build:dev`.

---

### What Not to Do

- Do not put JSON-LD in layout components (`PostLayout`, `BookLayout`). Layout components cannot know page-specific metadata, and putting structured data there would make it impossible to inject page-specific data without prop-drilling through the entire layout tree.
- Do not add JSON-LD to `src/app/layout.tsx` for a site-level WebSite schema. While valid, a WebSite schema adds little practical value for rich results compared to page-level schemas and is outside the stated requirements.
- Do not use `JSON.stringify(schema, null, 2)` (pretty-printed) in production — compact output reduces HTML payload size.
- Do not add the `@context` field to individual nodes inside `@graph` — it belongs only at the top level.

    18→    return { year, month, day };
    19→  });
    20→}

    24→export async function generateMetadata({ params }: { params: Promise<{ year: string; month: string; day: string }> }): Promise<Metadata> {

     2→import { notFound } from 'next/navigation';

     9→
    10→function safeDecodeParam(param: string): string {
    11→  try {
    12→    return decodeURIComponent(param);
    13→  } catch {
    14→    return param;
    15→  }
    16→}
    17→
    18→function resolvePostFromParam(rawSlug: string) {
    19→  const decoded = safeDecodeParam(rawSlug);
    20→  return (
    21→    getPostBySlug(decoded) ||
    22→    getPostBySlug(rawSlug) ||
    23→    getPostBySlug(decoded.normalize('NFC')) ||
    24→    getPostBySlug(decoded.normalize('NFD'))
    25→  );
    26→}
    27→
    28→export async function generateStaticParams() {
    29→  const params: { slug: string; postSlug: string }[] = [];
    30→
    31→  // Custom posts basePath — all posts served at /[basePath]/[slug]
    32→  const basePath = getPostsBasePath();
    33→  if (basePath !== 'posts') {
    34→    getAllPosts().forEach(post => { params.push({ slug: basePath, postSlug: post.slug }); });
    35→  }
    36→
    37→  // Series custom paths — only posts belonging to that series
    38→  for (const [seriesSlug, customPath] of Object.entries(getSeriesCustomPaths())) {
    39→    getSeriesPosts(seriesSlug).forEach(post => { params.push({ slug: customPath, postSlug: post.slug }); });
    40→  }
    41→
    42→  // Placeholder keeps Next.js happy with output: export when no custom paths configured.
    43→  // dynamicParams = false ensures any unrecognised slug/postSlug combo returns 404.
    44→  return params.length > 0 ? params : [{ slug: '_', postSlug: '_' }];
    45→}
    46→
    47→export const dynamicParams = false;
    48→
    49→export async function generateMetadata({
    50→  params,
    51→}: {
    52→  params: Promise<{ slug: string; postSlug: string }>;
    53→}): Promise<Metadata> {
    54→  const { postSlug: rawPostSlug } = await params;
    55→  const post = resolvePostFromParam(rawPostSlug);
    56→
    57→  if (!post) {
    58→    return { title: 'Post Not Found' };
    59→  }
    60→
    61→  const ogImage =
    62→    post.coverImage && !post.coverImage.startsWith('text:') && !post.coverImage.startsWith('./')
    63→      ? post.coverImage
    64→      : siteConfig.ogImage;
    65→
    66→  return {
    67→    title: `${post.title} | ${resolveLocale(siteConfig.title)}`,
    68→    description: post.excerpt,
    69→    openGraph: {
    70→      title: post.title,
    71→      description: post.excerpt,
    72→      type: 'article',
    73→      publishedTime: post.date,
    74→      authors: post.authors,
    75→      images: [
    76→        {
    77→          url: ogImage,
    78→          width: 1200,
    79→          height: 630,
    80→          alt: post.title,
    81→        },
    82→      ],
    83→      siteName: resolveLocale(siteConfig.title),
    84→    },
    85→    twitter: {
    86→      card: 'summary_large_image',
    87→      title: post.title,
    88→      description: post.excerpt,
    89→      images: [ogImage],
    90→    },
    91→  };
    92→}
    93→
    94→export default async function PrefixPostPage({
    95→  params,
    96→}: {
    97→  params: Promise<{ slug: string; postSlug: string }>;
    98→}) {
    99→  const { slug: prefix, postSlug: rawPostSlug } = await params;
   100→
   101→  // Validate the prefix is a known custom path
   102→  const basePath = getPostsBasePath();
   103→  const customPaths = getSeriesCustomPaths();
   104→  const isValidBasePath = prefix === basePath && basePath !== 'posts';
   105→  const matchedSeriesSlug = Object.entries(customPaths).find(([, path]) => path === prefix)?.[0];
   106→
   107→  if (!isValidBasePath && !matchedSeriesSlug) {
   108→    notFound();
   109→  }
   110→
   111→  const post = resolvePostFromParam(rawPostSlug);
   112→  if (!post) {
   113→    notFound();
   114→  }
   115→
   116→  const layout = post.layout || 'post';
   117→
   118→  if (layout === 'simple') {
   119→    return <SimpleLayout post={post} />;
   120→  }
   121→
   122→  const relatedPosts = getRelatedPosts(post.slug);
   123→  const { prev, next } = getAdjacentPosts(post.slug);
   124→  const slugRegistry = buildSlugRegistry();
   125→  const backlinks = getBacklinks(post.slug);
   126→  let seriesPosts: PostData[] = [];
   127→  let seriesTitle: string | undefined;
   128→
   129→  if (post.series) {
   130→    seriesPosts = getSeriesPosts(post.series);
   131→    const seriesData = getSeriesData(post.series);
   132→    seriesTitle = seriesData?.title;
   133→  }
   134→
   135→  return (
   136→    <PostLayout
   137→      post={post}
   138→      relatedPosts={relatedPosts}
   139→      seriesPosts={seriesPosts}
   140→      seriesTitle={seriesTitle}
   141→      prevPost={prev}
   142→      nextPost={next}
   143→      backlinks={backlinks}
   144→      slugRegistry={slugRegistry}
   145→    />
   146→  );
   147→}

  "content": "# Plan: SEO Boost — JSON-LD Structured Data\n\n## Context\n\nThe Amytis blog has solid OG/Twitter metadata but zero JSON-LD structured data. Adding\nJSON-LD `Article`, `Book`, and `BreadcrumbList` schemas unlocks Google rich results\n(article carousels, breadcrumb trails in SERPs) and improves crawlability. No new npm\npackages are needed — structured data is pure JSON injected via a `<script>` tag.\n\n---\n\n## Files\n\n| Action | Path |\n|--------|------|\n| **Create** | `src/lib/json-ld.ts` |\n| **Modify** | `src/app/posts/[slug]/page.tsx` |\n| **Modify** | `src/app/[slug]/[postSlug]/page.tsx` |\n| **Modify** | `src/app/books/[slug]/page.tsx` |\n| **Modify** | `src/app/books/[slug]/[chapter]/page.tsx` |\n| **Modify** | `src/app/flows/[year]/[month]/[day]/page.tsx` |\n\nExisting utilities reused:\n- `getPostsListUrl()` — `src/lib/urls.ts:29` — builds the posts listing URL\n- `getPostUrl(post)` — `src/lib/urls.ts:20` — canonical post URL (handles custom basePath)\n- `resolveLocale()` — `src/lib/i18n.ts` — already imported in all target pages\n\n---\n\n## Step 1 — Create `src/lib/json-ld.ts`\n\nPure TypeScript module (no React, no side-effects). Exports four builder functions that\naccept pre-resolved data and return a `SchemaGraph` object ready for `JSON.stringify`.\n\n### Schema injection pattern (Next.js App Router)\n```tsx\n<script\n  type=\"application/ld+json\"\n  dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: JSON.stringify(jsonLd) }}\n/>\n```\n\n### Output structure (all pages use `@graph`)\n```json\n{\n  \"@context\": \"https://schema.org\",\n  \"@graph\": [ ...one or more schema nodes... ]\n}\n```\n\n### Exported functions & params\n\n```ts\nexport function buildPostJsonLd(params: {\n  post: { title, excerpt, date, authors, tags, coverImage?, series? };\n  postUrl: string;          // absolute\n  seriesTitle?: string;     // if post belongs to a series\n  seriesUrl?: string;       // absolute URL to the series page\n  siteTitle: string;\n  siteUrl: string;          // no trailing slash\n  defaultOgImage: string;\n}): SchemaGraph\n\nexport function buildBookJsonLd(params: {\n  book: { title, excerpt?, date, authors, coverImage?, slug };\n  bookUrl: string;\n  siteTitle: string;\n  siteUrl: string;\n  defaultOgImage: string;\n}): SchemaGraph\n\nexport function buildBookChapterJsonLd(params: {\n > DEVELOPER

why need BreadcrumbList?

> AGENT

Good question. BreadcrumbList has one specific benefit: Google displays the breadcrumb path directly in search results instead of the raw URL.

**Without BreadcrumbList:**
```
https://example.com/posts/my-long-post-slug
```

**With BreadcrumbList:**
```
example.com › Series Title › Post Title
```

That's the entire value proposition — cosmetic improvement in SERPs that can improve click-through rate.

**Arguments against including it here:**

- The visual benefit is modest — most users click based on title and description, not the URL line
- This blog uses clean, readable slugs already (not `?id=12345` style)
- It adds complexity to the `buildPostJsonLd` function (series title lookup logic)
- It's the most implementation-effort part of the JSON-LD work

**My revised suggestion:** Drop BreadcrumbList. Focus only on:

- `BlogPosting` for posts and flows
- `Book` for book landing pages
- `Article` (with `isPartOf`) for book chapters

These directly enable **Article rich results** in Google — the carousel/featured snippets that actually drive traffic. Breadcrumbs are pure cosmetic.

Want me to revise the plan without BreadcrumbList?

> DEVELOPER

yes, drop BreadcrumbList. and there are two more concerns: why treat posts and flows together? why use Article for book chapters?

> AGENT

Good questions.

**Posts vs Flows:**

They're structurally different content:
- Posts are deliberate, edited articles with titles, tags, categories, authors
- Flows are daily notes — ephemeral, diary-like, no authors, no category

Google's `BlogPosting` schema expects `author` and structured editorial content. Applying it to flows is a stretch — flows are closer to `SocialMediaPosting` or just not worth marking up at all. The SEO value of flows in search results is also low (they're dated scratchpads, not indexable articles people search for).

**Recommendation:** Apply `BlogPosting` to posts only. Skip flows entirely.

---

**`Article` for book chapters:**

`Article` is technically correct since schema.org has no `Chapter` type with rich-result support. But the `isPartOf: Book` relationship is the part that matters — it tells Google the chapter belongs to a book. The problem is Google doesn't currently surface any rich results from `Article + isPartOf Book` combinations. It's valid schema but earns nothing visible in SERPs.

**Recommendation:** Keep book chapters if you want the semantic relationship expressed in structured data, but drop the expectation of rich results. Alternatively, skip chapters and only mark up the `Book` landing page — that's where Google would link anyway.

---

**Revised minimal scope:**
- `BlogPosting` for posts only
- `Book` for book landing pages
- Optionally: `Article + isPartOf` for book chapters (semantic value, no rich results)

How do you want to proceed?

> DEVELOPER

I am concerning the name, why not Post but BlogPosting? why not BookChapter or other name but Article?

> AGENT

Fair point — the naming is driven by schema.org, not our choice.

**`BlogPosting` vs `Article`:**

Both exist on schema.org. `BlogPosting` is a subtype of `Article`, specifically for blog posts. Google's rich results documentation accepts both. `BlogPosting` is more semantically precise for dated blog content, which is why I chose it. But `Article` works too and is more broadly recognized.

**`Article` for book chapters:**

schema.org has no `Chapter` type at all. The closest types are `Article`, `CreativeWork`, or nothing. I used `Article` as the least-bad fit. If you find `Article` semantically wrong for a chapter, the alternative is to just skip JSON-LD for chapters entirely — the `Book` landing page is where the real SEO value is.

---

**Summary of naming constraints:**

| Our concept | schema.org type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Post | `BlogPosting` or `Article` | Both valid; `BlogPosting` more specific |
| Book | `Book` | Exact match |
| Chapter | `Article` (with `isPartOf`) | No `Chapter` type exists |
| Flow | `BlogPosting` or skip | Debatable fit |

The types are schema.org vocabulary — we pick the closest available, not a perfect match.

Given this, do you want to:
1. Use `Article` for posts (simpler, broader) instead of `BlogPosting`
2. Skip chapters entirely (only mark up `Book` landing pages)
3. Keep the original plan (`BlogPosting` for posts, `Article` for chapters)

> DEVELOPER

3

No new npm packages\nare needed — structured data is pure JSON injected via a `<script>` tag.\n\n**Scope:** `BlogPosting` for posts, `Book` for book landing pages, `Article` (with `isPartOf`) for book chapters. Flows and BreadcrumbList are out of scope.\n\n---\n\n## Files\n\n| Action | Path |\n|--------|------|\n| **Create** | `src/lib/json-ld.ts` |\n| **Modify** | `src/app/posts/[slug]/page.tsx` |\n| **Modify** | `src/app/[slug]/[postSlug]/page.tsx` |\n| **Modify** | `src/app/books/[slug]/page.tsx` |\n| **Modify** | `src/app/books/[slug]/[chapter]/page.tsx` |\n\nExisting utilities reused:\n- `getPostUrl(post)` — `src/lib/urls.ts:20` — canonical post URL (handles custom basePath)\n- `resolveLocale()` — `src/lib/i18n.ts` — already imported in all target pages\n\n---\n\n## Step 1 — Create `src/lib/json-ld.ts`\n\nPure TypeScript module (no React, no side-effects). Exports three builder functions.\n\n### Schema injection pattern (Next.js App Router official pattern)\n```tsx\n<script\n  type=\"application/ld+json\"\n  dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: JSON.stringify(jsonLd) }}\n/>\n```\n\n### Output structure (all pages use `@graph`)\n```json\n{\n  \"@context\": \"https://schema.org\",\n  \"@graph\": [ ...one or more schema nodes... ]\n}\n```\n\n### Exported functions & params\n\n```ts\nexport function buildPostJsonLd(params: {\n  post: { title, excerpt, date, authors, tags, coverImage?, series? };\n  postUrl: string;       // absolute URL\n  siteTitle: string;\n  siteUrl: string;       // no trailing slash\n  defaultOgImage: string;\n}): SchemaGraph\n\nexport function buildBookJsonLd(params: {\n  book: { title, excerpt?, date, authors, coverImage?, slug };\n  bookUrl: string;       // absolute URL\n  siteTitle: string;\n  siteUrl: string;\n  defaultOgImage: string;\n}): SchemaGraph\n\nexport function buildBookChapterJsonLd(params: {\n  chapter: { title, excerpt?, slug };\n  book: { title, slug, date, authors };\n  chapterUrl: string;    // absolute URL\n  bookUrl: string;       // absolute URL\n  siteTitle: string;\n  siteUrl: string;\n}): SchemaGraph\n```\n\n### Schema per page type\n\n**Posts → `BlogPosting`**\n```json\n{\n  \"@type\": \"BlogPosting\",\n  \"headline\": \"Post Title\",\n  \"description\": \"excerpt\",\n  \"url\": \"https://example.com/posts/my-post\",\n  \"datePublished\": \"2026-01-01\",\n  \"dateModified\": \"2026-01-01\",\n  \"author\": [{ \"@type\": \"Person\", \"name\": \"Author\" }],\n  \"publisher\": { \"@type\": \"Organization\", \"name\": \"Site\", \"url\": \"https://example.com\" },\n  \"image\": { \"@type\": \"ImageObject\", \"url\": \"https://example.com/og.png\" },\n  \"keywords\": \"tag1, tag2\",\n  \"mainEntityOfPage\": { \"@type\": \"WebPage\", \"@id\": \"https://example.com/posts/my-post\" }\n}\n```\n\n**Books → `Book`**\n```json\n{\n  \"@type\": \"Book\",\n  \"name\": \"Book Title\",\n  \"description\": \"excerpt\",\n  \"url\": \"https://example.com/books/my-book\",\n  \"datePublished\": \"2026-01-01\",\n  \"author\": [{ \"@type\": \"Person\", \"name\": \"Author\" }],\n  \"publisher\": { \"@type\": \"Organization\", \"name\": \"Site\", \"url\": \"https://example.com\" },\n  \"image\": { \"@type\": \"ImageObject\", \"url\": \"https://example.com/og.png\" }\n}\n```\n\n**Book chapters → `Article` with `isPartOf`**\n```json\n{\n  \"@type\": \"Article\",\n  \"headline\": \"Chapter Title\",\n  \"description\": \"excerpt\",\n  \"url\": \"https://example.com/books/my-book/chapter-1\",\n  \"datePublished\": \"2026-01-01\",\n  \"author\": [{ \"@type\": \"Person\", \"name\": \"Author\" }],\n  \"publisher\": { \"@type\": \"Organization\", \"name\": \"Site\", \"url\": \"https://example.com\" },\n  \"isPartOf\": { \"@type\": \"Book\", \"@id\": \"https://example.com/books/my-book\", \"name\": \"Book Title\" }\n}\n```\n\n### Image resolution rule\nMirror existing `generateMetadata` logic: use `coverImage` only if it is an external\nHTTP URL; otherwise fall back to `defaultOgImage`. (`./`-relative and `text:` values\nare excluded as they are not externally accessible.)\n\n### Edge cases\n- `authors: []` → `author` field is an empty array (valid; data layer populates default authors)\n- `tags: []` → `keywords` field is `undefined`, omitted by `JSON.stringify`\n- `excerpt` missing on chapter → `description` is `undefined`, omitted by `JSON.stringify`\n\n---\n\n## Step 2 — `src/app/posts/[slug]/page.tsx`\n\n`seriesTitle` is resolved at line 123. Add JSON-LD after line 124 in the post-layout branch:\n\n```ts\nimport { buildPostJsonLd } from '@/lib/json-ld';\nimport { getPostUrl } from '@/lib/urls'; // already imported partially\n\nconst siteUrl = siteConfig.baseUrl.replace(/\\/+$/, '');\nconst jsonLd = buildPostJsonLd({\n  post,\n  postUrl: `${siteUrl}${getPostUrl(post)}`,\n  siteTitle: resolveLocale(siteConfig.title),\n  siteUrl,\n  defaultOgImage: siteConfig.ogImage,\n});\n```\n\nWrap the `return <PostLayout .../>` at line 127 in a fragment:\n```tsx\nreturn (\n  <>\n    <script type=\"application/ld+json\" dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: JSON.stringify(jsonLd) }} />\n    <PostLayout ... />\n  </>\n);\n```\n\nThe `layout === 'simple'` branch (line 109) is skipped — simple layout is for static pages.\n\n---\n\n## Step 3 — `src/app/[slug]/[postSlug]/page.tsx`\n\nIdentical to Step 2. `seriesTitle` is resolved at line 132.\nWrap the `return <PostLayout .../>` at line 135 in the same fragment pattern.\nThe `layout === 'simple'` branch (line 118) is skipped.\n\n---\n\n## Step 4 — `src/app/books/[slug]/page.tsx`\n\nAdd after the `notFound()` guard (line 57):\n\n```ts\nimport { buildBookJsonLd } from '@/lib/json-ld';\n\nconst siteUrl = siteConfig.baseUrl.replace(/\\/+$/, '');\nconst jsonLd = buildBookJsonLd({\n  book,\n  bookUrl: `${siteUrl}/books/${slug}`,\n  siteTitle: resolveLocale(siteConfig.title),\n  siteUrl,\n  defaultOgImage: siteConfig.ogImage,\n});\n```\n\nWrap the `return <div className=\"layout-main\">` at line 62 in a fragment.\n\n---\n\n## Step 5 — `src/app/books/[slug]/[chapter]/page.tsx`\n\nAdd after the `notFound()` guard (line 59):\n\n```ts\nimport { buildBookChapterJsonLd } from '@/lib/json-ld';\n\nconst siteUrl = siteConfig.baseUrl.replace(/\\/+$/, '');\nconst jsonLd = buildBookChapterJsonLd({\n  chapter, book,\n  chapterUrl: `${siteUrl}/books/${slug}/${chapterSlug}`,\n  bookUrl: `${siteUrl}/books/${slug}`,\n  siteTitle: resolveLocale(siteConfig.title),\n  siteUrl,\n});\n```\n\nWrap `return <BookLayout book={book} chapter={chapter} />` at line 61 in a fragment.\n\n---\n\n## Verification\n\n1. `bun run build:dev` — confirm no TypeScript errors\n2. View source of a post page → search `application/ld+json` → confirm `BlogPosting` node\n3. View source of a book chapter page → confirm `Article` node with `isPartOf`\n4. Google Rich Results Test — paste HTML snippet — should validate `BlogPosting`\n5. `bun test` — confirm no regressions\n"
}
```

> DEVELOPER

do you think make it configurable?

> AGENT

My take: **no, don't make it configurable.**

JSON-LD is unlike analytics or comments — it has no user-facing impact, no privacy concern, and no reason anyone would want to turn it off. It's invisible metadata that strictly helps search indexing.

Compare with features that *are* configurable in `site.config.ts`:
- `analytics.providers` → privacy-sensitive, some users actively opt out
- `comments.provider` → user-facing UI, not everyone wants comments
- `feed.format` → affects public endpoints, format preference matters

JSON-LD doesn't fit any of those reasons. Making it configurable would:
- Add a config field to both `site.config.ts` and `site.config.example.ts` (per the CLAUDE.md rule)
- Add a runtime check in every affected page
- Solve a problem that doesn't exist in practice

If a user of Amytis genuinely didn't want JSON-LD, they'd be an extreme edge case and could just remove the script tags themselves.

**Verdict:** Ship it unconditionally. Shall I proceed with implementation?

> DEVELOPER

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/amytis-claude-review/src/lib/json-ld.ts",
  "content": "// ── Schema shape types ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\ninterface SchemaOrganization {\n  '@type': 'Organization';\n  name: string;\n  url: string;\n}\n\ninterface SchemaPerson {\n  '@type': 'Person';\n  name: string;\n}\n\ninterface SchemaImageObject {\n  '@type': 'ImageObject';\n  url: string;\n}\n\ninterface SchemaBlogPosting {\n  '@type': 'BlogPosting';\n  headline: string;\n  description: string | undefined;\n  url: string;\n  datePublished: string;\n  dateModified: string;\n  author: SchemaPerson[];\n  publisher: SchemaOrganization;\n  image: SchemaImageObject;\n  keywords: string | undefined;\n  mainEntityOfPage: { '@type': 'WebPage'; '@id': string };\n}\n\ninterface SchemaBook {\n  '@type': 'Book';\n  name: string;\n  description: string | undefined;\n  url: string;\n  datePublished: string;\n  author: SchemaPerson[];\n  publisher: SchemaOrganization;\n  image: SchemaImageObject | undefined;\n}\n\ninterface SchemaArticle {\n  '@type': 'Article';\n  headline: string;\n  description: string | undefined;\n  url: string;\n  datePublished: string;\n  author: SchemaPerson[];\n  publisher: SchemaOrganization;\n  isPartOf: { '@type': 'Book'; '@id': string; name: string };\n}\n\ntype SchemaNode = SchemaBlogPosting | SchemaBook | SchemaArticle;\n\ninterface SchemaGraph {\n  '@context': 'https://schema.org';\n  '@graph': SchemaNode[];\n}\n\n// ── Helpers ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\nfunction buildPublisher(siteTitle: string, siteUrl: string): SchemaOrganization {\n  return { '@type': 'Organization', name: siteTitle, url: siteUrl };\n}\n\nfunction buildAuthors(names: string[]): SchemaPerson[] {\n  return names.map(name => ({ '@type': 'Person', name }));\n}\n\n/**\n * Resolve an absolute image URL for structured data.\n * Mirrors the cover-image logic used in generateMetadata across all page components:\n * use coverImage only if it is an external HTTP URL, otherwise fall back to defaultOgImage.\n */\nfunction resolveImageUrl(\n  coverImage: string | undefined,\n  defaultOgImage: string,\n  siteUrl: string,\n): string {\n  if (coverImage && coverImage.startsWith('http')) {\n    return coverImage;\n  }\n  return defaultOgImage.startsWith('http') ? defaultOgImage : `${siteUrl}${defaultOgImage}`;\n}\n\nfunction wrapGraph(nodes: SchemaNode[]): SchemaGraph {\n  return { '@context': 'https://schema.org', '@graph': nodes };\n}\n\n// ── Public param types ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\nexport interface PostJsonLdParams {\n  post: {\n    title: string;\n    excerpt: string;\n    date: string;\n    authors: string[];\n    tags: string[];\n    coverImage?: string;\n  };\n  postUrl: string;\n  siteTitle: string;\n  siteUrl: string;\n  defaultOgImage: string;\n}\n\nexport interface BookJsonLdParams {\n  book: {\n    title: string;\n    excerpt?: string;\n    date: string;\n    authors: string[];\n    coverImage?: string;\n  };\n  bookUrl: string;\n  siteTitle: string;\n  siteUrl: string;\n  defaultOgImage: string;\n}\n\nexport interface BookChapterJsonLdParams {\n  chapter: {\n    title: string;\n    excerpt?: string;\n  };\n  book: {\n    title: string;\n    date: string;\n    authors: string[];\n  };\n  chapterUrl: string;\n  bookUrl: string;\n  siteTitle: string;\n  siteUrl: string;\n}\n\n// ── Builders ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\nexport function buildPostJsonLd(params: PostJsonLdParams): SchemaGraph {\n  const { post, postUrl, siteTitle, siteUrl, defaultOgImage } = params;\n  const base = siteUrl.replace(/\\/+$/, '');\n\n  const node: SchemaBlogPosting = {\n    '@type': 'BlogPosting',\n    headline: post.title,\n    description: post.excerpt || undefined,\n    url: postUrl,\n    datePublished: post.date,\n    dateModified: post.date,\n    author: buildAuthors(post.authors),\n    publisher: buildPublisher(siteTitle, base),\n    image: { '@type': 'ImageObject', url: resolveImageUrl(post.coverImage, defaultOgImage, base) },\n    keywords: post.tags.length > 0 ? post.tags.join(', ') : undefined,\n    mainEntityOfPage: { '@type': 'WebPage', '@id': postUrl },\n  };\n\n  return wrapGraph([node]);\n}\n\nexport function buildBookJsonLd(params: BookJsonLdParams): SchemaGraph {\n  const { book, bookUrl, siteTitle, siteUrl, defaultOgImage } = params;\n  const base = siteUrl.replace(/\\/+$/, '');\n  const imageUrl = resolveImageUrl(book.coverImage, defaultOgImage, base);\n\n  const node: SchemaBook = {\n    '@type': 'Book',\n    name: book.title,\n    description: book.excerpt || undefined,\n    url: bookUrl,\n    datePublished: book.date,\n    author: buildAuthors(book.authors),\n    publisher: buildPublisher(siteTitle, base),\n    image: imageUrl ? { '@type': 'ImageObject', url: imageUrl } : undefined,\n  };\n\n  return wrapGraph([node]);\n}\n\nexport function buildBookChapterJsonLd(params: BookChapterJsonLdParams): SchemaGraph {\n  const { chapter, book, chapterUrl, bookUrl, siteTitle, siteUrl } = params;\n  const base = siteUrl.replace(/\\/+$/, '');\n\n  const node: SchemaArticle = {\n    '@type': 'Article',\n    headline: chapter.title,\n    description: chapter.excerpt || undefined,\n    url: chapterUrl,\n    datePublished: book.date,\n    author: buildAuthors(book.authors),\n    publisher: buildPublisher(siteTitle, base),\n    isPartOf: { '@type': 'Book', '@id': bookUrl, name: book.title },\n  };\n\n  return wrapGraph([node]);\n}\n"
}

Done. Here's a summary of what was implemented:

---

**New file: `src/lib/json-ld.ts`**
- `buildPostJsonLd` → `BlogPosting` schema with headline, description, dates, authors, publisher, image, keywords, mainEntityOfPage
- `buildBookJsonLd` → `Book` schema
- `buildBookChapterJsonLd` → `Article` schema with `isPartOf: Book`

**Modified pages (5 files):**
- `src/app/posts/[slug]/page.tsx` — `BlogPosting` on post layout branch
- `src/app/[slug]/[postSlug]/page.tsx` — `BlogPosting` for custom basePath/series posts
- `src/app/books/[slug]/page.tsx` — `Book`
- `src/app/books/[slug]/[chapter]/page.tsx` — `Article` with `isPartOf`

Each page injects a single `<script type="application/ld+json">` tag with a `@graph` wrapper. You can verify with Google's Rich Results Test after a build.

     1→# Amytis
     2→
     3→[English](README.md) | [简体中文](README.zh.md)
     4→
     5→**Amytis** is an elegant, open-source framework for building a personal digital garden: a living knowledge space where ideas grow from raw notes to refined writing. It is built with Next.js 16, React 19, and Tailwind CSS v4, with a strong focus on readability, structure, and long-term content ownership.
     6→
     7→[**Live Demo**](https://amytis.vercel.app/)
     8→
     9→![Amytis Screenshot](public/images/amytis-screenshot.jpg)
    10→
    11→## The Knowledge Ladder
    12→
    13→Amytis is built around a simple path from rough to refined:
    14→
    15→- **Flow**: Capture raw daily thoughts and fragments.
    16→- **Articles**: Refine one idea into a clear essay.
    17→- **Series**: Connect related articles into a curated narrative.
    18→- **Books**: Distill mature knowledge into structured chapters and parts.
    19→
    20→Each stage builds on the previous one, so your garden can evolve naturally.
    21→
    22→## Features
    23→
    24→- **Digital Garden Philosophy:** Non-linear navigation through tags, series, authors, books, flows, and chronological archives.
    25→- **Interconnected Knowledge:**
    26→  - **Wiki-links:** Bidirectional linking (`[[Slug]]`) between all content types.
    27→  - **Backlinks:** Automatic "Linked References" display on notes.
    28→  - **Knowledge Graph:** Interactive visual map of your content connections.
    29→- **Full-text Search:** Fast, static client-side search across all content (Cmd/Ctrl+K) powered by Pagefind.
    30→- **Structured Content:**
    31→  - **Series:** Multi-part content organization with manual or automatic ordering.
    32→  - **Books:** Long-form content with explicit chapters, parts, and a dedicated reading interface.
    33→  - **Notes:** Atomic, evergreen concepts for personal knowledge management.
    34→  - **Flows:** Stream-style daily notes or micro-blogging for quick thoughts.
    35→- **Rich MDX Content:**
    36→  - GitHub Flavored Markdown (tables, task lists, strikethrough).
    37→  - Syntax-highlighted code blocks.
    38→  - Mermaid diagrams (flowcharts, sequence diagrams, etc.).
    39→  - LaTeX math via KaTeX.
    40→  - Raw HTML support for custom layouts.
    41→- **Elegant Design:**
    42→  - Minimalist aesthetic with high-contrast typography.
    43→  - Light/Dark mode with automatic system detection.
    44→  - Four color palettes: default (emerald), blue, rose, amber.
    45→  - Responsive layout optimized for reading.
    46→  - Horizontal scrolling for featured content on the homepage.
    47→- **Table of Contents:** Sticky TOC with scroll tracking, reading progress indicator, and active heading highlight.
    48→- **Flexible Content Structure:**
    49→  - Flat files (`post.mdx`) or nested folders (`post/index.mdx`).
    50→  - Co-located assets: keep images inside post folders (`./images/`).
    51→  - Date-prefixed filenames: `2026-01-01-my-post.mdx`.
    52→  - Draft support for posts, series, books, and flows.
    53→- **Performance & SEO:**
    54→  - Fully static export with optimized WebP images.
    55→  - Native sitemap and RSS feed generation.
    56→  - Multilingual reading time estimate (supports Latin and CJK).
    57→- **Integrations:**
    58→  - Analytics: Umami, Plausible, or Google Analytics.
    59→  - Comments: Giscus (GitHub Discussions) or Disqus.
    60→  - Internationalization: multi-language support (en, zh) with localized `site.config.ts`.
    61→- **Content CLI Tools:** Create posts, series, and import from PDFs or image folders.
    62→- **Modern Stack:** Next.js 16, React 19, Tailwind CSS v4, TypeScript 5, Bun.
    63→
    64→## Design Philosophy
    65→
    66→- **Elegance by default**: Typography, spacing, and color should feel intentional out of the box.
    67→- **Content over configuration**: Writing and publishing should be simple file-based workflows, not CMS-heavy setup.
    68→- **Markdown-first, not markdown-limited**: Keep authoring portable while supporting rich output (math, diagrams, code, wikilinks).
    69→- **Ship what you need**: Features are modular through `site.config.ts`; disable sections you do not use.
    70→- **Plain text, long-term ownership**: Content stays in Markdown/MDX so it remains versionable and portable.
    71→
    72→## Quick Start
    73→
    74→1. **Install Dependencies:**
    75→   ```bash
    76→   bun install
    77→   ```
    78→
    79→2. **Start Development Server:**
    80→   ```bash
    81→   bun dev
    82→   ```
    83→   Visit [http://localhost:3000](http://localhost:3000).
    84→
    85→3. **Build for Production (Static Export):**
    86→   ```bash
    87→   bun run build
    88→   ```
    89→   The static site will be generated in the `out/` directory with optimized images.
    90→
    91→4. **Development Build (faster, no image optimization):**
    92→   ```bash
    93→   bun run build:dev
    94→   ```
    95→
    96→## CLI Commands
    97→
    98→```bash
    99→## Core
   100→bun dev
   101→bun run lint
   102→bun run validate
   103→
   104→## Build & Deploy
   105→bun run build
   106→bun run build:dev
   107→bun run clean
   108→bun run deploy                 # Deploy to Linux/nginx server (requires .env.local)
   109→
   110→## Test
   111→bun test
   112→bun run test:unit
   113→bun run test:int
   114→bun run test:e2e
   115→
   116→## Create Content
   117→bun run new "Post Title"
   118→bun run new-series "Series Name"
   119→bun run new-note "Concept"
   120→bun run new-flow
   121→
   122→## Import / Maintain
   123→bun run new-from-pdf ./doc.pdf
   124→bun run new-from-images ./photos
   125→bun run new-flow-from-chat
   126→bun run import-book
   127→bun run sync-book
   128→bun run series-draft "series-slug"
   129→```
   130→
   131→### Importing Chat Logs to Flows
   132→
   133→Drop `.txt` or `.log` files into `imports/chats/`, then run:
   134→
   135→```bash
   136→bun run new-flow-from-chat
   137→```
   138→
   139→Common flags: `--all`, `--dry-run`, `--author "Name"`, `--append`, `--timestamp`.
   140→Import history is stored in `imports/chats/.imported`.
   141→
   142→## Configuration
   143→
   144→All site settings are managed in `site.config.ts`:
   145→
   146→```typescript
   147→export const siteConfig = {
   148→  // ...
   149→  nav: [
   150→    { name: "Home", url: "/", weight: 1 },
   151→    { name: "Flow", url: "/flows", weight: 1.1 }, // Add Flows to nav
   152→    { name: "Series", url: "/series", weight: 1.5 },
   153→    { name: "Books", url: "/books", weight: 1.7 },
   154→    { name: "Archive", url: "/archive", weight: 2 },
   155→    // ...
   156→  ],
   157→  // ...
   158→  flows: {
   159→    recentCount: 5,
   160→  },
   161→};
   162→```
   163→
   164→## Writing Content
   165→
   166→### Posts
   167→
   168→Create `.md` or `.mdx` files in `content/posts/`.
   169→
   170→### Flows
   171→
   172→Create daily notes in `content/flows/YYYY/MM/DD.mdx` or `content/flows/YYYY/MM/DD/index.mdx`.
   173→
   174→### Series
   175→
   176→Create a directory in `content/series/` with an `index.mdx`.
   177→
   178→### Books
   179→
   180→Books are for long-form, structured content. Create a directory in `content/books/`.
   181→
   182→### Notes
   183→
   184→Create evergreen notes in `content/notes/` (e.g., `concept.mdx`). Use `[[wiki-links]]` to connect them.
   185→
   186→## Project Structure
   187→
   188→```
   189→amytis/
   190→  content/
   191→    posts/              # Blog posts
   192→    series/             # Series collections
   193→    books/              # Long-form books
   194→    notes/              # Digital garden notes
   195→    flows/              # Daily notes (YYYY/MM/DD)
   196→    about.mdx           # Static pages
   197→  public/               # Static assets
   198→  src/
   199→    app/                # Next.js App Router pages
   200→      books/            # Book routes
   201→      notes/            # Note routes
   202→      graph/            # Knowledge graph
   203→      flows/            # Flow routes
   204→    components/         # React components
   205→    lib/
   206→      markdown.ts       # Data access layer
   207→  site.config.ts        # Site configuration
   208→```
   209→
   210→## Documentation
   211→
   212→- [Architecture Overview](docs/ARCHITECTURE.md)
   213→- [Deployment Guide](docs/deployment.md)
   214→- [Digital Garden Guide](docs/DIGITAL_GARDEN.md)
   215→- [Contributing Guide](docs/CONTRIBUTING.md)
   216→
   217→## License
   218→
   219→MIT
   220→

     1→# Amytis
     2→
     3→[English](README.md) | [简体中文](README.zh.md)
     4→
     5→**Amytis** 是一个优雅的开源数字花园框架，用于构建个人知识空间。它基于 Next.js 16、React 19 和 Tailwind CSS v4，强调可读性、结构化表达与长期内容所有权。
     6→
     7→[**在线演示**](https://amytis.vercel.app/)
     8→
     9→![Amytis 截图](public/images/amytis-screenshot.jpg)
    10→
    11→## 知识阶梯
    12→
    13→Amytis 围绕一条从粗糙到精炼的知识路径构建：
    14→
    15→- **Flow（随笔）**：记录每日想法与碎片。
    16→- **Articles（文章）**：将单个想法打磨成清晰文章。
    17→- **Series（系列）**：把相关文章串联为一条主题叙事。
    18→- **Books（书籍）**：将成熟知识沉淀为章节化结构。
    19→
    20→每个阶段都建立在上一阶段之上，花园会自然生长。
    21→
    22→## 功能特性
    23→
    24→- **数字花园体验**：通过标签、系列、作者、书籍、Flow 和归档进行非线性导航。
    25→- **互联知识网络**：
    26→  - **双向链接**：支持 `[[Slug]]` 跨内容类型关联。
    27→  - **反向链接**：在笔记页自动显示“Linked References”。
    28→  - **知识图谱**：以可视化方式展示内容连接关系。
    29→- **全文搜索**：基于 Pagefind 的静态搜索，支持 Cmd/Ctrl+K 快捷键。
    30→- **结构化内容体系**：
    31→  - **Series**：支持手动或自动排序的多篇集合。
    32→  - **Books**：支持章节与分部的长篇阅读界面。
    33→  - **Notes**：原子化常青笔记。
    34→  - **Flows**：流式日记/微记录。
    35→- **富文本 MDX 能力**：
    36→  - GitHub Flavored Markdown（表格、任务列表等）
    37→  - 代码高亮
    38→  - Mermaid 图表
    39→  - KaTeX 数学公式
    40→  - 原生 HTML 支持
    41→- **阅读体验与设计**：
    42→  - 高可读排版与响应式布局
    43→  - 自动系统主题检测（明/暗）
    44→  - 四套配色主题：default、blue、rose、amber
    45→  - 吸顶目录与阅读进度跟踪
    46→- **性能与 SEO**：
    47→  - 全静态导出与 WebP 优化
    48→  - 自动 sitemap 与 RSS
    49→  - 支持 Latin/CJK 的多语言阅读时长估算
    50→- **集成能力**：
    51→  - 统计：Umami / Plausible / Google Analytics
    52→  - 评论：Giscus / Disqus
    53→  - i18n：`site.config.ts` 中配置多语言（en / zh）
    54→
    55→## 设计理念
    56→
    57→- **默认优雅**：排版、间距、色彩开箱即用且有审美一致性。
    58→- **内容优先**：通过文件化写作与发布流程完成创作，不依赖重型 CMS。
    59→- **Markdown 优先但不受限**：保持可迁移写作体验，同时支持数学、图表、代码和双向链接。
    60→- **按需启用**：`site.config.ts` 提供模块化开关，仅启用你需要的能力。
    61→- **纯文本长期所有权**：内容存储于 Markdown/MDX，便于版本管理与长期迁移。
    62→
    63→## 快速开始
    64→
    65→1. **安装依赖**
    66→   ```bash
    67→   bun install
    68→   ```
    69→
    70→2. **启动开发环境**
    71→   ```bash
    72→   bun dev
    73→   ```
    74→   打开 [http://localhost:3000](http://localhost:3000)。
    75→
    76→3. **生产构建（静态导出）**
    77→   ```bash
    78→   bun run build
    79→   ```
    80→   产物位于 `out/` 目录。
    81→
    82→4. **开发构建（更快，无图片优化）**
    83→   ```bash
    84→   bun run build:dev
    85→   ```
    86→
    87→## CLI 命令
    88→
    89→```bash
    90→## Core
    91→bun dev
    92→bun run lint
    93→bun run validate
    94→
    95→## Build & Deploy
    96→bun run build
    97→bun run build:dev
    98→bun run clean
    99→bun run deploy                 # 部署到 Linux/nginx 服务器（需要 .env.local 配置）
   100→
   101→## Test
   102→bun test
   103→bun run test:unit
   104→bun run test:int
   105→bun run test:e2e
   106→
   107→## Create Content
   108→bun run new "Post Title"
   109→bun run new-series "Series Name"
   110→bun run new-note "Concept"
   111→bun run new-flow
   112→
   113→## Import / Maintain
   114→bun run new-from-pdf ./doc.pdf
   115→bun run new-from-images ./photos
   116→bun run new-flow-from-chat
   117→bun run import-book
   118→bun run sync-book
   119→bun run series-draft "series-slug"
   120→```
   121→
   122→### 导入聊天记录到 Flows
   123→
   124→将 `.txt` 或 `.log` 文件放入 `imports/chats/` 后执行：
   125→
   126→```bash
   127→bun run new-flow-from-chat
   128→```
   129→
   130→常用参数：`--all`、`--dry-run`、`--author "Name"`、`--append`、`--timestamp`。  
   131→导入历史记录位于 `imports/chats/.imported`。
   132→
   133→## 配置
   134→
   135→所有站点配置集中在 `site.config.ts`。
   136→
   137→## 内容写作
   138→
   139→- **Posts**：创建到 `content/posts/`
   140→- **Flows**：创建到 `content/flows/YYYY/MM/DD.mdx`（或文件夹模式）
   141→- **Series**：创建 `content/series/<slug>/index.mdx`
   142→- **Books**：创建到 `content/books/<slug>/`
   143→- **Notes**：创建到 `content/notes/`，支持 `[[wiki-links]]`
   144→
   145→## 项目结构
   146→
   147→```text
   148→amytis/
   149→  content/
   150→    posts/
   151→    series/
   152→    books/
   153→    notes/
   154→    flows/
   155→  public/
   156→  src/
   157→    app/
   158→    components/
   159→    lib/
   160→  site.config.ts
   161→```
   162→
   163→## 文档
   164→
   165→- [架构说明](docs/ARCHITECTURE.md)
   166→- [部署指南](docs/deployment.md)
   167→- [数字花园指南](docs/DIGITAL_GARDEN.md)
   168→- [贡献指南](docs/CONTRIBUTING.md)
   169→
   170→## 许可证
   171→
   172→MIT
   173→

</details>

**Escape `<` characters in JSON-LD before injecting into `<script>` tags.**

`JSON.stringify()` does not escape HTML characters. If the stringified JSON contains `<` (e.g., from frontmatter like `description: "</script><img>"`), the HTML parser will interpret `</script>` as a closing tag before the JSON parser processes it, breaking the script block and creating an injection vector. Escape `<` to `\u003c` before assignment.

This pattern appears in 4 files:
- src/app/posts/[slug]/page.tsx:139
- src/app/books/[slug]/page.tsx:73
- src/app/books/[slug]/[chapter]/page.tsx:74
- src/app/[slug]/[postSlug]/page.tsx:147

<details>
<summary>Proposed fix example</summary>

```diff
  const jsonLd = buildBookJsonLd({...});
+ const safeJsonLd = JSON.stringify(jsonLd).replace(/</g, '\\u003c');
  
  return (
    <>
-     <script type="application/ld+json" dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: JSON.stringify(jsonLd) }} />
+     <script type="application/ld+json" dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: safeJsonLd }} />
```
</details>

<details>
<summary>🧰 Tools</summary>

<details>
<summary>🪛 Biome (2.4.4)</summary>

[error] 73-73: Avoid passing content using the dangerouslySetInnerHTML prop.

(lint/security/noDangerouslySetInnerHtml)

</details>

</details>

<details>
<summary>🤖 Prompt for AI Agents</summary>

```

the script tag using dangerouslySetInnerHTML currently uses
JSON.stringify(jsonLd) which doesn't escape '<' and can break the script block;
update the code that sets the script's __html (the JSON.stringify call for
variable jsonLd used with dangerouslySetInnerHTML) to escape all '<' characters
(e.g., replace '<' with '\u003c' in the stringified output) before assigning it
to __html so the JSON-LD cannot prematurely close the script tag.

_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_

**`resolveImageUrl` currently drops valid site-relative cover images.**

`coverImage` values like `/books/...` or `/posts/...` (already normalized in parsing) are valid but ignored because the check only accepts `http*`. This causes JSON-LD to incorrectly fall back to `defaultOgImage` for many pages.



<details>
<summary>💡 Proposed fix</summary>

```diff
 function resolveImageUrl(
   coverImage: string | undefined,
   defaultOgImage: string,
   siteUrl: string,
 ): string {
-  if (coverImage && coverImage.startsWith('http')) {
-    return coverImage;
-  }
-  return defaultOgImage.startsWith('http') ? defaultOgImage : `${siteUrl}${defaultOgImage}`;
+  const toAbsolute = (url: string) =>
+    url.startsWith('http') ? url : `${siteUrl}${url.startsWith('/') ? url : `/${url}`}`;
+
+  if (coverImage && !coverImage.startsWith('text:') && !coverImage.startsWith('./')) {
+    return toAbsolute(coverImage);
+  }
+  return toAbsolute(defaultOgImage);
 }
```
</details>

```suggestion
function resolveImageUrl(
  coverImage: string | undefined,
  defaultOgImage: string,
  siteUrl: string,
): string {
  const toAbsolute = (url: string) =>
    url.startsWith('http') ? url : `${siteUrl}${url.startsWith('/') ? url : `/${url}`}`;

  if (coverImage && !coverImage.startsWith('text:') && !coverImage.startsWith('./')) {
    return toAbsolute(coverImage);
  }
  return toAbsolute(defaultOgImage);
}
```

</details>

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/amytis-claude-review/src/lib/json-ld.ts",
  "old_string": "/**\n * Resolve an absolute image URL for structured data.\n * Mirrors the cover-image logic used in generateMetadata across all page components:\n * use coverImage only if it is an external HTTP URL, otherwise fall back to defaultOgImage.\n */\nfunction resolveImageUrl(\n  coverImage: string | undefined,\n  defaultOgImage: string,\n  siteUrl: string,\n): string {\n  if (coverImage && coverImage.startsWith('http')) {\n    return coverImage;\n  }\n  return defaultOgImage.startsWith('http') ? defaultOgImage : `${siteUrl}${defaultOgImage}`;\n}\n\nfunction wrapGraph(nodes: SchemaNode[]): SchemaGraph {\n  return { '@context': 'https://schema.org', '@graph': nodes };\n}",
  "new_string": "/**\n * Resolve an absolute image URL for structured data.\n * Accepts external URLs (http/https) and site-relative paths (/books/..., /posts/...).\n * Rejects relative paths (./images/...) and text placeholders (text:...).\n */\nfunction resolveImageUrl(\n  coverImage: string | undefined,\n  defaultOgImage: string,\n  siteUrl: string,\n): string {\n  const toAbsolute = (url: string) =>\n    url.startsWith('http') ? url : `${siteUrl}${url.startsWith('/') ? url : `/${url}`}`;\n\n  if (coverImage && !coverImage.startsWith('text:') && !coverImage.startsWith('./')) {\n    return toAbsolute(coverImage);\n  }\n  return toAbsolute(defaultOgImage);\n}\n\n/**\n * Serialize a JSON-LD graph to a string safe for injection into a <script> tag.\n * JSON.stringify does not escape '<', so a value containing '</script>' could\n * break the script block. Replacing '<' with '\\u003c' prevents this.\n */\nexport function serializeJsonLd(schema: SchemaGraph): string {\n  return JSON.stringify(schema).replace(/</g, '\\\\u003c');\n}\n\nfunction wrapGraph(nodes: SchemaNode[]): SchemaGraph {\n  return { '@context': 'https://schema.org', '@graph': nodes };\n}"
}
